Un origen S3 falso local tampoco sirve: mediría la política CORS del falso, que es exactamente el argumento con el que la 3.13 descartó probar contra `batch()`, «una prueba que informa sobre código que nadie despacha». **Lo que tendría que ser cierto, contado y no estimado:** (1) las credenciales de R2 como secretos del repositorio —hoy el job de e2e las omite A PROPÓSITO y su propio comentario lo dice, porque sus rutas fallan cerradas sin ellas y eso es lo que sus especificaciones miden—; (2) una sesión autenticada en el navegador, que la suite no sabe fabricar: `publish-access.spec.ts` prueba la NEGATIVA, y entrar exigiría un proveedor de credenciales de prueba o una cookie de sesión firmada con `AUTH_SECRET`; (3) la cookie de borrador del paso 1 para llegar al paso 2; (4) un barrendero para los objetos reales que cada corrida dejaría en el bucket — la 3.13 ya registra que los derivados huérfanos no los limpia nadie.

  **Y una corrección al texto de esta tarea, que es el hallazgo que importa: aun con los cuatro puntos, esta prueba NO habría atrapado el defecto del 2026-08-18.** La política que `docs/going-live.md` transcribe ya permite `http://localhost:3000` y `https://*.vercel.app` — o sea los dos orígenes desde los que puede correr CI. El defecto real fue que **el origen de PRODUCCIÓN** no estaba en la lista, y un navegador que sube desde un origen permitido contesta verde mientras ese hueco sigue abierto. Es la trampa número uno de este cambio: una prueba en verde bajo la mutación exacta que existía para atrapar. Lo que sí lo atraparía es afirmar la CONFIGURACIÓN del bucket (`GetBucketCors`) contra el origen de producción — sin navegador, sin sesión y sin subir nada. Queda como **3.19**
- [ ] 3.19 **(nueva, 2026-08-28 — sale de reintentar la 3.17.)** Afirmar la política CORS del bucket, no la subida: `GetBucketCors` sobre el bucket de producción y `expect(AllowedOrigins).toContain(<origen de producción>)`, más `AllowedMethods: ["PUT"]` y `AllowedHeaders: ["content-type"]`, que es lo que la firma presignada obliga a que el `OPTIONS` acepte. **Es la afirmación que el 2026-08-18 habría puesto en rojo y que un navegador corriendo desde un origen ya permitido no puede hacer.** No se construye todavía porque necesita las credenciales de R2 en CI, y shippearla sin ellas sería exactamente el defecto que la 8.9 acaba de barrer: código que no ejecuta nadie. Va con el trabajo de despliegue, junto a la 3.17 y `docs/going-live.md` **The only test that can catch a CORS failure**, and the reason it is its own task: photo upload shipped with 42 green specs across four files and was impossible in production, because the browser blocked every PUT before it left. `upload-request` and `compress` are pure, `r2-photo-storage` injects its S3 client, `process-uploaded-photo` uses fakes — **not one of them makes a real request**, and none of them can, because CORS is a browser policy that does not exist server-side. Needs the R2 credentials in CI and a session, so it belongs with the deploy work rather than before it. `docs/going-live.md` carries the configuration this depends on
- [x] 3.16 Design update of 2026-08-18, imported from the founder's Claude Design project (`Rentas - Sistema.dc.html`, artboard `2g`). **The colour half has shipped**: `--warn` and `--warn-bg` repainted from the palette itself — the mustard pair was the only colour on the publish screen belonging to no theme — plus `--meta`, a new token for monospaced metadata (counts, file sizes, step labels) declared separately from `--disp` because they share a value under `compacto` and mean different things. **`oscuro` was redrawn from menta's own palette**, `--surface` being menta's accent navy and `--accent` its mint, replacing an amber theme that shared nothing with it; the theme-contract gate needed narrowing for `--r`/`--rs`, which the new dark theme deliberately keeps identical because a radius is geometry rather than colour, and the narrowed rule was proven to still catch a real colour leak. **Artboard `2g` shipped too**, and it was a different screen from the one that had shipped: per-photo rows carrying a thumbnail, the file name and its compression result (`2,4 MB → 38 KB`), a `Portada` badge on the first, a per-photo remove button, per-photo states for compressing and for refusal (`✱ Es un video, no una foto`), a `4 de 6` counter, `+ Agregar más`, drag-to-reorder, an empty state, and the trust block that says publishing photos taken from another advert costs the account. **The design says "Hasta 8 fotos" and the founder chose to keep 6** (2026-08-18): eight would cost about a quarter of the free tier's catalogue capacity — ~5,900 listings against ~7,900 — measured against the stored derivatives rather than the discarded originals. The screen's copy therefore reads 6, and reads it **from `MAX_PHOTOS_PER_LISTING`** rather than repeating a number — a copy that states a limit it does not share with the validator is a copy that goes stale. Two tokens were added for it: `--meta-fs-sm` (11px, the step label and the counter) and `--r-thumb` (6px, because a 12px round on a 64×48 image eats the image). **The corner-radius gate needed fixing to accept it**: `border-radius: 0 var(--r-thumb) 0 var(--r-thumb)` was reported as a literal because the check read the whole value rather than each part, and the repaired check now also catches a literal hiding among tokens (`0 6px 0 var(--r)`), which the old one missed entirely

### A second forecast correction, measured 2026-08-17

The Phase 1b note above revised estimates upward once, for proof. **Phase 3's UI slices show the same error again in a different place: a form is not one unit of work per field.** `PublishForm` is 528 authored lines for eight fields — 241 component, 136 proof, 91 styles, 60 route — and that is *after* extracting a shared `Field` renderer that removed 36 lines of duplication. Nothing in it is padding: every field carries a real `<label>`, help text, a per-field error region, `aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`, and the submitted value round-tripping so a phone user does not retype what they already wrote.

The lesson is not "grant another exception". It is that **a screen with N form fields costs roughly 55–65 authored lines per field including proof and styles**, so any remaining form surface must be forecast from its field count and split before it is built — never estimated as one slice and then rescued with an exception. Applied below to PR9's import preview and PR7's renewal surfaces.
- [x] 3.10 RED: after upload, only derivatives are persisted — the original file is not retained (D12) — `deriveListingPhoto` returns exactly two members and the source is not one of them. **This is the half this layer can enforce, not all of D12**: the R2 adapter (3.7) must still refuse to PUT the buffer it was handed, and that is a separate guarantee
- [x] 3.11 GREEN: `sharp` emits a row thumbnail at 128 × 96 (≤ 10 KB — covers the 44 × 34 mobile and 64 × 48 desktop row at 2× under `compacto`, D12/D14) and a detail image (≤ 200 KB) at upload; both stored in R2; platform on-demand image optimization is not used — WebP for both. **The byte budget is met by construction, not asserted afterwards**: quality steps down, and when quality alone cannot reach it the longest edge steps down too (1280 → 1024 → 800). That ladder exists because the first implementation threw instead, and the noise fixtures proved that was reachable — a maximally-detailed 1280-edge image lands at ~209 KB even at quality 40. Refusing a publisher's upload because their photograph carried too much detail is a broken product. Measured against incompressible noise: thumbnail **0.1–0.5 KB** (budget 10 KB), detail **167–190 KB** (budget 200 KB). **Recorded rather than quietly accepted: the thumbnail budget has ~20× headroom and therefore cannot fail — it is not a real constraint.** Storing the bytes in R2 belongs to 3.7
- [x] 3.12 RED: derivative dimensions and byte budgets hold for a portrait, a landscape, and an oversized source photo — plus the pixel-bomb bound promised in 3.6, landed here because it needs the decoder (40 MP; a 12 MP phone photo is 4032 × 3024). **The fixtures are deterministic random noise, and that is the load-bearing choice**: a solid-colour test image compresses to almost nothing, so a byte budget asserted against one passes however badly the encoder is configured — a gate that cannot fail, which is the failure this project has already shipped five times. A measured curiosity worth keeping: the 12 MP source fits at 1280px while a 1600 × 900 source falls back to 1024, because downscaling further averages the noise away and yields a *more* compressible image

## Phase 4: Trust — Photo-Hash Dedup (PR4)

**The pure half was pulled forward**, split across two slices — `trust/perceptual-hash-core` (dHash + Hamming, merged as #14) and `trust/perceptual-hash-adapter` (the port, the sharp boundary and the calibration harness). The reason for pulling it forward is the **uncalibrated `<= 8` threshold** (see Open Questions): the harness has to exist before real photographs can decide the number, and no amount of testing substitutes for that data. Tasks 4.1, 4.6, 4.7 and the `PublishListingUseCase` wiring all need `listing`, which is PR3.

- [x] 4.1 Schema: `listing_photo_hash` (`bit(64)`) — **`bit(64)`, not `bigint` and not `text`.** The similarity query is `bit_count(hash # $1) <= $2`, Postgres's own population count over an XOR, and that operator pair exists only for bit strings: storing this as a number would force every comparison into application code — the sequential-scan-in-TypeScript D4 exists to avoid — and storing it as text would make the same query a lie that happens to parse. Drizzle has no first-class `bit`, so the type is declared with `customType` rather than approximated with one that ships. `photo_id` is the primary key, because a photo with two hashes makes "is this a duplicate" depend on which row a scan reached first. **There is deliberately no `publisher_id` column**, even though the duplicate query filters on it: it is reachable by joining `listing_photo` to `listing`, and a copy here would be a second source of truth for who owns a listing — the exact fact D4's same-publisher exemption rests on. A denormalised copy that drifts turns "this is your own photo, republish freely" into a false accusation of duplication, which is the worst failure this feature has. **4.6's drift cross-check ships with the schema rather than after it**: the domain's own `KNOWN_HAMMING_DISTANCE_VECTORS` are replayed through a real `SELECT bit_count($1::bit(64) # $2::bit(64))` and asserted three ways — the declared value, what TypeScript computes, what Postgres computes
- [x] 4.2 RED: cross-publisher perceptually-matching photo rejects the listing. **Proved against `PhotoHashPort` with an in-memory fake, not the real adapter** (`application/ports/photo-hash.port.test.ts`) — the database behaviour is not proven until 4.6
- [x] 4.3 RED: same-publisher match (active/expired/other listing) is allowed — same fake, same caveat
- [x] 4.4 GREEN (hash computation only): `sharp` 9×8 grayscale dHash(64) — pure algorithm in `domain/dhash.ts`, sharp confined to `infrastructure/sharp-dhash.ts`. Wiring into `PublishListingUseCase` needs `listing` (PR3)
- [x] 4.5 GREEN: `PhotoHashPort` exposing only `findMatchesFromOtherPublishers(hash, excludePublisherId, maxDistance)` — no all-matches method (D4)
- [x] 4.6 GREEN: Drizzle/raw-SQL adapter using `bit_count` Hamming distance. `bit_count(a # b)` is Postgres's own population count over an XOR, so the scan runs where the rows are instead of pulling every hash into a serverless function to loop over. **The same-publisher exemption is not a filter this adapter remembers to apply**: the port exposes one query and `excludePublisherId` is required on it, so the exclusion reaches the SQL by construction — an owner republishing their own property after expiry passes by the shape of the API, not by anyone's discipline. `publisher_id` is **joined, never copied**, because a copy would be a second source of truth for who owns a listing and a drifted copy turns "this is your own photo" into a false accusation. **The drift cross-check landed with 4.1**: the domain's `KNOWN_HAMMING_DISTANCE_VECTORS` replayed through real `bit_count`, asserted three ways. The integration test proves what an in-memory fake structurally cannot — the fake filters because it was written to, this proves the SQL does — with Ana's expired listing deliberately holding the SAME hash as Bruno's, so a missing or misapplied exclusion returns two rows instead of one
- [x] 4.7 E2E: publish → duplicate photo rejected cross-account, accepted same publisher. **The task text understated the work, and this correction records why.** By the time this ran, PR3 had long shipped and 4.5/4.6 were both green, but grep across `src/` still found zero callers of `PhotoHashPort.findMatchesFromOtherPublishers` and zero writers of `listing_photo_hash` outside its own adapter test (`process-uploaded-photo.test.ts`, `attach-photo-to-draft.ts`'s own "known gap" docstring, and 9.20-9.23's own note). "E2E" alone would have proven a check that could structurally never fire, against a table nothing had ever written to. Actually closing the gap needed three things this line never named: (1) `PhotoHashPort.record(photoId, hash, recordedAt)` — the write half added beside the existing read method, deliberately NOT `publisherId`-bearing, since `listing_photo_hash` has no such column (schema.ts) and a copy would drift; (2) the hash computed and the cross-account check run inside `processUploadedPhoto` — the ONE choke point `publishListing` and `attachPhotoToDraft` both already call — fail-closed, so a photo whose hash cannot be computed is refused outright; (3) the actual `record()` call, placed in each caller AFTER its own listing/photo row exists, because `listing_photo_hash.photo_id` is a primary key referencing `listing_photo.id` — recording earlier is not just premature, it would risk poisoning the table against a publisher whose submission was then rejected for an unrelated reason. `ListingRepositoryPort.save` and `ListingPhotoAttachmentPort.attachPhoto` both widened their return types to hand back the real persisted photo id(s), in submission order, for exactly this reason. The Hamming threshold landed as `MAX_DUPLICATE_HAMMING_DISTANCE = 8` in the domain (hamming-distance.ts) — the design.md Open Question's own proposed, uncalibrated value, deliberately NOT marked resolved. Proven against real Postgres in `tests/integration/photo-duplicate-rejection.test.ts`: a photo matching another publisher's is rejected; the same publisher's own photo — on an active OR an expired listing — is accepted; a photo matching nothing is accepted and its hash is actually recorded (the one assertion that would have silently passed as false before this task, since the table was permanently empty)

## Phase 5: Listing Search (PR5)

- [x] 5.0 RED: a submitted `zone` that does not belong to the submitted `city` is ignored, not treated as a meaningful filter. **Found while building the cascading select in PR2b:** a GET form submits whatever its controls currently hold, so picking a new city without touching the zone sends the previous city's zone. Nothing is written, so D5's database constraint is not involved and cannot help — this one has to be handled where the query parameters are read, and the component cannot prevent it because it does not control what the browser posts — **the subtle one.** A GET form posts whatever its controls hold, so a stale city/zone pair reaches the server whenever someone changes the city without touching the zone. The zone is dropped by comparing it against the SUBMITTED city, not against the caller's list. **Open and unbuilt**: the drop is silent, so the 5.7 UI has no way to say "mostrando toda la ciudad" — a `droppedZone` flag is the obvious fix, left for whoever builds the reader
- [x] 5.1 RED: `ListingSearchPort.search(criteria)` — missing/nullable `cityId` rejected (D5)
- [x] 5.2 GREEN: search port signature with required non-nullable `cityId` — `search(criteria)` is the only method; there is no `searchAll` and no sentinel. **Stated at its real strength**: this holds while the interface has one method and nothing stops an adapter from ignoring `cityId` once inside, which is why the integration test exists. Mutation-checked: making `cityId` optional fails `tsc` with three unused-`@ts-expect-error` errors, so CI enforces it
- [x] 5.3 RED: integration — Maracaibo search excludes Distrito Capital listings (no-filter, wide-price-range, colliding-zone-name scenarios)
- [x] 5.4 GREEN: Drizzle search query — city/zone/price/characteristics filters — `city_id` and `status = 'active'` are appended before any caller-supplied filter, and `listing_city_status_idx` is `(city_id, status)`, so the pair every query starts with is also its access path
- [x] 5.5 RED: expired and auto-hidden listings excluded from search
- [x] 5.6 GREEN: active-only status filter — mutation-checked at the SQL level: deleting the predicate removes `status` from the generated `WHERE`
- [x] 5.7 Search results UI showing `publisher_type` per result — artboard 2a, and **it is one control set rendered two ways rather than two components**: a row of chips on a phone, a 240px sidebar at 1280, both the same `<form method="get">`. The GET is the whole mechanism (D11/D13): every change reloads with the state in the URL, which makes a filtered search **linkable, shareable and indexable** — and that matters more than usual here because listings circulate by WhatsApp, so a filter nobody can paste into a chat is a filter that does not travel. **The submit button is visible and required**: there is no `onChange` to auto-submit, and hiding it would leave the form unusable for exactly the visitors the no-JS rule exists for. Nothing is preselected, because a default city would silently answer D5's question for the visitor and they would never learn the catalogue has two cities. The last room step reads **`4+`, never a bare `4`** — a segmented control whose last step quietly meant "exactly four" would hide every larger apartment from the people most likely to want one. An empty result says what to change rather than leaving a blank column that reads as broken, and no city at all asks for one instead of showing a list that quietly crosses cities. Measured at both widths: every control is a real 44px target at 360, and the filter column never overflows — four room chips plus two price inputs in a row is the likeliest way that breaks, and a sideways-scrolling filter panel is one nobody finishes. **The results ARE the root** (founder, 2026-08-19): there is no separate home page, and the design supports that by omission — it has no home artboard and never mentions one. A landing page that only links onward spends the domain's strongest URL on a click, and in a classifieds product that URL belongs to the listings. `/alquiler/<ciudad>/<zona>/<slug>-<id>` stays the listing scheme. **Recorded as a cost, not discovered later**: there is now nowhere for a value proposition or a publish pitch beyond the bar's button. And an addition to the design, stated as one: artboard 2a gives this screen no heading at all, which is fine for a results page reached from elsewhere and wrong for a site root, so a visually hidden `<h1>` carries the document outline while the screen looks exactly as drawn

## Phase 6: Contact Reveal (PR6)

- [x] 6.1 Migration: `contact_reveal_event` table + `contact_reveal_unique_pair` VIEW (raw SQL, D6) — the table is append-only and **all four foreign keys are `ON DELETE restrict`, deliberately**. Cascading would delete the go/pivot evidence at exactly the moment a listing is taken down or an account closed. The cost is real and is the point: an account-erasure request now fails loudly until someone decides between anonymising and dropping these rows, which is design.md's own open question made visible instead of silently resolved the wrong way
- [x] 6.2 Hand-declare TS result type for the view query (flag as drift risk in code comment) — **the drift risk, recorded above `UniquePairRow`**: `drizzle-kit` neither creates nor diffs a view, and `db.execute` returns untyped rows, so the TS type is a hand declaration the compiler cannot check. Drift does not crash — a renamed column arrives as `undefined`, `Number(undefined)` is `NaN` — so the failure mode is a go/pivot report reading `NaN` unique pairs. The integration test is the only thing pinning it. `pgView` was considered and rejected: `DISTINCT ON` plus a window `count(*)` still needs an `.as(sql\`…\`)` escape hatch, which moves the unchecked string rather than removing it
- [x] 6.3 RED: anonymous visitor sees hidden/locked placeholder, no contact value
- [x] 6.4 RED: reveal creates exactly one event; repeat reveal by same tenant creates a second, non-deduplicated event
- [x] 6.5 GREEN: `RevealContactUseCase` — single insert, session-gated — session read **before** the listing is touched, mutation-checked: moving the gate after the read turns exactly that ordering test red
- [x] 6.6 RED: integration — after N repeat reveals of one pair, unique-pair view returns 1 row, `reveal_count=N`, `first_revealed_at`=earliest; raw event table still holds N rows — proven against real Postgres 18. **Verified independently rather than on report**: recreating the view without `DISTINCT ON` turns four tests red, including the one whose assertion the agent had itself tightened from `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual` to `toBeGreaterThan` after discovering the loose form passed against the broken view
- [x] 6.7 GREEN: view query wrapper
- [x] 6.8 Reveal UI: locked placeholder, reveal button, sign-in redirect. **Hecho 2026-08-22, PR #81.** `ContactBlock` con sus tres estados, `reveal-actions.ts` como acción de servidor (no un enlace: hay que registrar el evento que cuenta la métrica norte), `DrizzleRevealableListing`, y `view-listing-contact.ts` para el lado de lectura. El destino de vuelta pasó a ser una regla del dominio (`identity/domain/safe-return-destination.ts`) al descubrir que la comparación de prefijo dejaba la acción como un redirector abierto

  > **Correction landed 2026-08-21, ahead of 6.8.** The contact-reveal module modelled the publisher contact as a single `whatsapp: string`, written when no column stored one. Two columns ship now — `listing.contact_method` and `listing.contact_value`, both NOT NULL, copied at publish time — and `publishable-listing.ts` had already recorded the rule they exist for: "the reveal button's label comes from this, so a listing that says 'Ver WhatsApp' while holding an address is a promise the product does not keep." The method never reached the presentation layer, so 6.8's button would have said "Ver WhatsApp del dueño" over an email address for every publisher who chose one. Fixed in the domain rather than in the component, because the component is being redesigned and the rule is not: `ContactPresentation` now carries `method` in BOTH branches and `value` in the revealed branch only — **the method survives the lock, the value cannot be represented inside it**, which is the guarantee 6.3 was written for and it is unchanged. `contactChannelNoun` returns the channel noun alone ("WhatsApp" / "teléfono" / "email") so the sentence around it stays in the UI layer. Two mutations checked: making the locked branch carry the value turned 4 tests red including the `not.toContain` leak assertions, and hard-coding the noun to "WhatsApp" turned 1 red. **Open for the founder, four words wide**: SISTEMA.md writes the label as "Ver WhatsApp del dueño" and gives no wording for a broker or for the other two channels — the noun is derived, the rest of the sentence is not yet decided
- [x] 6.9 RED: an account holding **40 distinct listings inside a rolling 24-hour window** is refused the 41st, and the refusal writes no reveal event and discloses no contact value — the catalog must not be drainable by one registered account. **The unit is the listing, not the action**: a second reveal of a listing already counted inside the window must leave the allowance untouched, so the test that matters is the one where a tenant re-opens the same advert and is not charged for it. **Done 2026-08-24.** `evaluateRevealAllowance` unit RED confirmed missing module, then `revealContact` use-case RED confirmed (rate limit not enforced) before any implementation existed — see `reveal-rate-limit.test.ts` and the rate-limit cases in `reveal-contact.test.ts`
- [x] 6.10 GREEN: per-account reveal rate limit in the domain — **40 distinct listings / rolling 24h**, resolved by the founder 2026-08-24 (design.md Open Questions). The window rolls, it does not reset at midnight: an account refused at 18:00 recovers allowance as its oldest reveals age past 24 hours, never in a nightly batch. The rule lives in `contact-reveal/domain`, never in the component. **Done.** `reveal-rate-limit.ts` (`evaluateRevealAllowance`, pure — no `Date` at all, the rolling window lives in the new `RevealRateLimitPort` read query) wired into `revealContact`; `DrizzleContactRevealEvents` now also implements `RevealRateLimitPort` (`findRecentlyRevealedListingIds`) against the same table, no second repository. Integration-tested against real Postgres in `tests/integration/contact-reveal.test.ts`
- [x] 6.11 Migration: `contact_reveal_event.message` as **nullable `text`, plus a `NOT VALID` CHECK constraint** — not `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`. Rows already exist from PR #81, and the two obvious migrations are both wrong here. A plain `NOT NULL` fails outright against them; `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` succeeds by **writing a fact that never happened** into an append-only log, recording that those tenants submitted an empty message when the product had not yet asked them for one. This table is the go/pivot evidence and 6.1 already refused `ON DELETE cascade` for the same reason: history is not edited to make a schema convenient. **Done.** `drizzle/0012_icy_spectrum.sql` — the column comes from `drizzle-kit generate`; the `NOT VALID` CHECK is hand-appended (drizzle-kit does not emit `NOT VALID`) with the exact SQL below, plus the "never VALIDATE this" warning written into the migration file itself. Integration-tested: a `NULL`-message row (simulating a PR #81 row) still inserts; a blank/whitespace message is refused by Postgres

  ```sql
  ALTER TABLE contact_reveal_event ADD COLUMN message text;
  ALTER TABLE contact_reveal_event
    ADD CONSTRAINT contact_reveal_event_message_present
    CHECK (message IS NOT NULL AND length(btrim(message)) > 0) NOT VALID;
  ```

  **`NOT VALID` is the whole point and it is permanent, not a to-do.** Postgres skips the check for rows that already exist and enforces it on every insert and update from that moment on — which is exactly "the rule starts now". The constraint must never be `VALIDATE`d: validating it would fail against the historical rows, and passing would mean someone backfilled them. Record that in the migration itself, or the next person reads `NOT VALID` as unfinished work and finishes it.

  `NULL` therefore means one thing only: **this reveal predates the requirement.** It is not an empty message and cannot be confused with one, because `btrim` rejects blank and whitespace-only text at the database level, under the constraint and independently of the domain rule in 6.13. Every read of `message` handles `NULL` as the pre-rule case — including the abuse signal, which must not read a historical row as a suspicious blank submission. `contact_reveal_unique_pair` stays untouched: the message is carried, never aggregated
- [x] 6.12 RED: a reveal submitted without a message is refused — **no event recorded and no contact value returned**. Both halves matter: an event written for a refused reveal would inflate the north-star metric with contacts nobody made. **Done.** `reveal-message.test.ts` RED confirmed (module missing) before `requireRevealMessage` existed; `reveal-contact.test.ts`'s `it.each(["", "   ", undefined])` case RED-confirmed the use case still disclosed the contact before the wiring landed
- [x] 6.13 GREEN: the message is required, validated and stored on the reveal event by `revealContact`. **Stored at reveal time on purpose** — `wa.me` prefilled text is editable by the visitor and no messaging application reports back what was actually sent, so our row is the evidence and the prefill is only convenience. Trusting WhatsApp here would leave a decorative field with neither protection nor abuse signal. **Done.** `reveal-message.ts` (`requireRevealMessage`) checked via `btrim`-equivalent (matches the DB constraint's rule exactly) but returns the ORIGINAL untrimmed text — the spec calls it "the authoritative record of what the tenant wrote", so validation decides blank-or-not without rewriting what was submitted. Wired into `revealContact` right after the session gate, before the listing is touched (pure check, no I/O)
- [x] 6.14 GREEN: `contactActionHref` carries the tenant's own message instead of the fixed sentence hard-coded at `ContactBlock.tsx:181`. The domain already builds `https://wa.me/<intl>?text=<encoded>` and already normalises Venezuelan numbers (`0412…` → `58412…`), so this is a change of argument, not of mechanism. **Done end-to-end, closed 2026-08-24.** `ContactPresentation`'s revealed branch and `ContactViewer` carry an optional `message`; `presentContact` passes it through. The read-back gap flagged in the first pass is now closed: `view-listing-contact.ts` reads the tenant's message back via a new `RevealMessageHistoryPort.findLatestMessage(tenantUserId, listingId)` — kept alongside `RevealRateLimitPort` rather than weakening the write-only `ContactRevealEventPort`, implemented by `DrizzleContactRevealEvents` (same table, same class as the rate-limit read) with `ORDER BY revealed_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1`, because repeat reveals are never deduplicated (task 6.4) and the LATEST one is the one the tenant meant. Wired into `page.tsx` alongside the existing `reveals`/`listings` ports, fetched in parallel with the listing lookup — one extra read, paid only once `hasRevealed` is already known true. `NULL` (a reveal predating the migration) round-trips as `null` without crashing; `ContactBlock`'s existing `contact.message ?? defaultRevealMessage(listingTitle)` fallback still covers that case and the anonymous/never-revealed case. **Verified the locked branch leaks neither the key nor the read**: `ContactPresentation`'s `locked`/`expired` variants have no `message` field at the type level, and `view-listing-contact.test.ts` asserts `messages.findLatestMessage` is never called for an anonymous visitor or a signed-in visitor who has not revealed. Integration-tested against real Postgres: latest-of-two-messages wins, never-revealed returns `null`, and a historical `NULL` row (reproduced the same DROP/INSERT/ADD-CONSTRAINT-NOT-VALID way as the 6.11 constraint tests) returns `null` without error
- [x] 6.15 Reveal UI: message textarea **inside the `<form action={revealAction}>` that already exists** — it is a native POST that works without JavaScript, so the message needs no new endpoint, no client state and no new plumbing. The contact stays an `<ActionLink>` anchor rather than a scripted `window.open`, which is what makes the handoff survive a mobile popup blocker. **Done.** Reused the existing `Field`/textarea molecule (`components/molecules/Field.tsx`) inside the locked-state form, pre-filled with a sensible default the tenant can edit, `required` for the no-JS browser-level guard; `revealListingContact` reads `formData.get("message")` and passes it to `revealContact`, catching `MissingRevealMessageError`/`RevealRateLimitExceededError` the same way it already caught `ListingNotRevealableError` — a refusal re-renders the still-locked ficha instead of a broken error page

## Phase 7: Lifecycle — Expiry & Reminder Job (PR7)

- [x] 7.1 Schema: `listing.status`/`expires_at`; `listing_reminder` UNIQUE; `job_run`. **Reconciled 2026-08-24 — built, plan was stale.** All three live in `src/shared/db/schema.ts`. **Deviation from this task's own text, and it is a correction rather than a defect:** the unique key is `listing_reminder_cycle_unique` on **`(listing_id, kind, expires_at)`** — three columns, not two. `kind` had to enter the key because one cycle sends TWO distinct notices (expiry and photo purge); with the two-column key the purge notice would collide with the expiry notice of the same cycle and silently never send. Proven by `tests/integration/lifecycle.test.ts` — "la segunda reserva del mismo correo pierde" and "el correo de purga del mismo ciclo se reserva aparte"
- [x] 7.2 RED: listing expires 30 days after publish/last renewal, whichever later. **Reconciled 2026-08-24.** `domain/expiry.test.ts`: "vence 30 días después de publicar cuando nunca se renovó", "cuenta desde la renovación cuando es posterior a la publicación", "ignora una renovación anterior a la publicación" — the third is the one that pins *whichever later*
- [x] 7.3 GREEN: expiry calculation in domain. **Reconciled 2026-08-24.** `src/modules/listing-lifecycle/domain/expiry.ts`, with the listing's lifetime declared in one place ("declara la vida del aviso en un solo lugar") and the boundary pinned on both sides: not expired at the exact instant, expired one millisecond later
- [x] 7.4 RED: unauthenticated `POST /api/jobs/expiry-reminders` returns 401, `reminders_sent=0`. **Reconciled 2026-08-24.** `app/api/jobs/expiry-reminders/route.test.ts` covers three refusals, not one: no credential, wrong bearer, and **no `CRON_SECRET` on the server** — the last is the deploy that forgets the variable and would otherwise leave the route open to the world
- [x] 7.5 GREEN: constant-time Bearer `CRON_SECRET` check on job route. **Reconciled 2026-08-24.** `domain/cron-authorization.ts` — **in the domain, not the route**, so the second job route calls the same check instead of copy-pasting a `===` into it. Compares `timingSafeEqual` over the HMAC of each side, so the comparison never depends on the presented length, and **fails closed** when the server secret is missing. `app/api/jobs/photo-purge/route.ts` is the second caller that decision was made for
- [x] 7.6 RED: double-run of reminder job does not double-send (unique constraint). **Reconciled 2026-08-24.** `send-lifecycle-notices.test.ts` "una segunda corrida no vuelve a mandar el mismo correo" at the use-case level, and `tests/integration/lifecycle.test.ts` proves it against real Postgres — the constraint, not the application check, is what holds
- [x] 7.7 GREEN: `SendExpiryRemindersUseCase` — batched window select, insert `listing_reminder`, send email, record `job_run` (counts + failures). **Reconciled 2026-08-24**, shipped as `application/send-lifecycle-notices.ts`. Eight covering tests: the reservation is taken before the send and **returned on failure so the row can be retried** ("una falla del correo devuelve la reserva y no detiene los demás"), a publisher with no address counts as a failure rather than vanishing, listings are marked expired **before** deciding who to notify, and the run is recorded in `job_run` even when there was nothing to do. **Caveat, and it is the same one that blocks 7.11:** the only `LifecycleMailerPort` adapter is `console-lifecycle-mailer.ts`. The use case is complete and tested against a fake; nothing has ever left the machine
- [x] 7.8 RED: replayed renewal token rejected; GET on renewal link never mutates `expires_at`. **Reconciled 2026-08-24.** `renew-listing.test.ts` "rechaza el mismo token usado por segunda vez" and "muestra la confirmación sin mover nada"; an invalid token "no llega ni a leer el aviso", so a forged link costs no database read
- [x] 7.9 GREEN: HMAC-signed, listing-scoped, single-use, expiring token; GET renders confirmation, POST renews +30 days and burns token. **Reconciled 2026-08-24.** `domain/renewal-token.ts` with nine tests covering the attacks by name: a body rewritten to point at another listing, a token signed with a different secret, a signed body that is not JSON, and a signed listing id that is not a string. **An expired forged token is rejected by the signature, not by the date** — the order matters, because checking the date first would leak that the signature was valid. Two cycles of the same listing produce different tokens, which is what makes a token single-use
- [x] 7.10 RED: expired listing retained (not deleted), excluded from search, still renewable. **Reconciled 2026-08-24.** All three proven in `tests/integration/lifecycle.test.ts` against real Postgres: "marca vencido sin borrar la fila", "sale de la búsqueda", "sigue siendo renovable, y renovarlo lo devuelve a la búsqueda" — the round trip, not just the exclusion. A fourth test pins that a listing hidden by moderation is **not** touched by the expiry sweep
- [x] 7.11 Resend renewal notice; `vercel.json` cron schedule. **Closed 2026-08-24, the day the founder added the Resend key.** `infrastructure/resend-lifecycle-mailer.ts` replaces `ConsoleLifecycleMailer`, which is deleted — leaving a class whose own log line said "NO SE ENVIÓ ningún correo" would now be a lie. One instantiation site changed (`app/api/jobs/expiry-reminders/route.ts`).

  **No React Email template, and that is a decision rather than a shortcut.** The port had already ruled it out in writing: it takes a subject and a body that `composeNotice` built in the domain, because "un puerto que recibiera el aviso y el tipo de correo pondría la redacción del lado del proveedor". A template here would force one of two bad things — duplicate the copy, or move it out of the domain, which is the founder's permanent rule. What the adapter does instead is **presentation without wording**: `renderLifecycleHtml` splits the domain's body into paragraphs and escapes it, deciding not one word of what it says. Escaping is not cosmetic — the listing title was written by a person and travels inside that HTML, under our sending address.

  **Fails closed twice over.** `readMailerConfig` demands key AND sender together (a key without a sender bounces everything; a sender without a key sends nothing), and the route answers `500 mailer_not_configured` with `reminders_sent: 0` rather than starting a batch it cannot deliver — starting one would take each listing's ledger reservation, burn it with no mail sent, and the next run would find them reserved and never retry. It answers 500 and not 401 deliberately: the bearer was correct, so a 401 would send someone to rotate an unrelated secret.

  **`RESEND_API_KEY` alone is not enough**: `LIFECYCLE_MAIL_FROM` must be set and its domain verified in Resend, or every send bounces. Both are in `.env.example`. Four mutations checked red: removing the route guard, accepting a key without a sender, ignoring the error Resend RETURNS rather than throws, and dropping the HTML escape.

  **Proven to deliver, not just to compile — 2026-08-24.** A single real send through the configured adapter reached a Gmail inbox, in Primary and not in spam. **Stated at its real strength:** the sending domain is the founder's personal `gianbarboza.com` (tracked debt — `rentas.com.ve` is not owned yet), and mail from a domain you control to your own inbox is the easy case. It proves the wiring, the credentials and the HTML end to end; it does not prove deliverability to strangers, which only real volume will. The display name is set (`Rentas <avisos@…>`) so the recipient reads the product, not the domain — the mitigation that costs nothing while the address is provisional.
- [x] 7.12 E2E: reminder job → renewal link → renew flow. **CERRADA 2026-08-28: el camino feliz ya corre.** Lo que faltaba no era voluntad sino tres cosas que el repositorio no tenía: un aviso en una base alcanzable, su `expires_at` exacto —que es la cerradura del compare-and-swap— y el mismo `RENEWAL_TOKEN_SECRET` con el que firma el servidor. **La 11.22 puso las dos primeras** (`neonConfig.fetchEndpoint` contra `scripts/neon-http-proxy.mjs` más un Postgres sembrado) y el job de CI ya definía la tercera desde la 7.11; nadie lo había vuelto a mirar. `tests/e2e/renewal-link.spec.ts` → **«un enlace firmado renueva 30 días, y el mismo enlace no renueva dos veces»**, cuatro afirmaciones que sólo se pueden hacer con un aviso de verdad: el `GET` muestra el aviso LEÍDO (su título llega al HTML) y **no mueve la fila** —lo que la prueba de los dos `GET` no podía ver, porque sin base no había fila que mirar—; el `POST` suma 30 días contados desde AHORA y sube `expired` a `active`; y el mismo enlace contesta **409** la segunda vez sin mover la fila otro mes.

  **La fila la siembra y la borra la propia prueba, no `seed-e2e.ts`, y es una decisión y no una comodidad.** Renovar MUTA. Colgarse del catálogo compartido dejaría una suite que pasa según quién corrió antes; peor, `crawlability` corre en paralelo con `chromium` y encontraría el token ya quemado, leyendo «ya se usó» donde esperaba «renovado». Cada proyecto siembra **su propio id** (`idDePrueba(testInfo.project.name)`) y lo borra en el `finally`: sale igual con un reintento de CI, con los dos proyectos a la vez y corriendo dos veces seguidas. Ninguna fecha es literal — todas se derivan del reloj del momento, que es la trampa que ya mordió a este repositorio con una fixture de fecha escrita.

  **Tres mutaciones, y la tercera corrige lo que yo mismo iba a afirmar de más.** (a) `409` → `400` en la respuesta de «ya está renovado» de `app/renovar/[token]/route.ts`: **1.870 pruebas unitarias en verde** y sólo esta especificación en rojo, en los dos proyectos — la ruta entera (códigos, copia, encadenado) no la mide nadie más. (b) Quitar `eq(listings.expiresAt, request.expectedExpiresAt)` del `WHERE` de `DrizzleLifecycleListings.renew`, o sea convertir el token de un solo uso en uno infinito: **las 1.870 unitarias siguen verdes** y esto se pone rojo. (c) **Pero la integración TAMBIÉN atrapa la (b)** — `tests/integration/lifecycle.test.ts` falla 1 de 270 —, así que **no es cierto que esta especificación sea la única guardia del uso único**, y se anota en vez de dejar la frase bonita. Lo que sí es exclusivamente suyo es la (a): que la PANTALLA de renovación conteste lo que contesta.

  ~~**PARTIAL — el happy path sigue abierto**~~

  **What shipped, and why this half is the half a browser is for.** Four specs proving that a signed link arriving by email, opened the way a person opens it, **does not renew anything by being opened** and is not indexed: a token that does not parse and one with a plausible shape and a forged signature both refuse and never render the `<form method="post">` that is the only path to a renewal; the page carries `noindex, nofollow` on the error responses too, because those are the ones that circulate; and two consecutive raw `GET`s answer identically, which is what proves the open did not consume the token. Two mutations checked red: making the refusal render the form, and dropping the robots tag. **Runs everywhere with no `test.skip`** — an invalid token is rejected by the signature before the listing is ever read, so the whole path is exercised against the local fallback build pointed at an unroutable database.

  **What did NOT ship: running the reminder job end to end.** Since 7.11 wired `ResendLifecycleMailer`, running that job in CI would send **real email on every run** to whatever addresses are seeded — a scheduled sender, not a test. That half stays where it can be deterministic and offline: `send-lifecycle-notices.test.ts` and `tests/integration/lifecycle.test.ts`.

  **What remains, and what it needs.** The successful renewal in a browser — confirmation page, POST, listing back in search — needs two things Playwright cannot reach today: a listing seeded in the deployment's own database, and that deployment's `RENEWAL_TOKEN_SECRET` to mint a signature that verifies. Both mean new test-seeding infrastructure, which is its own work unit and not this one. Closing this task honestly is blocked on that, not on effort.

  `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains a fake `RENEWAL_TOKEN_SECRET` for the e2e job — it signs nothing real and exists so the local fallback can REJECT a forged token rather than answer 500 "falta el secreto", which is the weaker half of what the spec is there to prove

## Phase 8: Trust — Reporting & Auto-Hide (PR8)

**El «6/6» de esta fase era falso, y así es como se veía.** Las seis tareas
originales (8.1-8.6) estaban hechas de verdad: el caso de uso, el dominio, el
esquema, los adaptadores y la ruta de operador. Lo que ninguna tarea nombraba
era la interfaz del inquilino, así que la fase se podía marcar completa con
`reportListing` **sin un solo llamador** — capacidad construida, probada contra
Postgres real e inalcanzable desde un navegador. La cuenta correcta de esta
fase es 8/8 y no 6/6, y las dos que faltaban son la 8.7 (hecha) y la 8.8
(abierta). AGENTS.md §5 lo dice con otras palabras: una casilla puede mentir.

- [x] 8.1 Schema: `listing_report`, `moderation_action`. **`drizzle/0013_giant_shriek.sql`.** `listing_report`: `UNIQUE(listing_id, reporter_id)` IS the "at most one report per account" guarantee — the same "constraint, not an `if`" reasoning `listing_reminder_cycle_unique` already established — so `count(*) WHERE listing_id = $1` is already a distinct-account count by construction. `moderation_action` has no `actor` column: this project has no operator-account model (the restore route is gated by a shared bearer secret, not a signed-in `user` row), so there is no identity to reference honestly. Both tables are `ON DELETE restrict` on every FK, same reasoning as `contact_reveal_event` (design.md D6): a report and a moderation action are both evidence, and `CASCADE` would erase exactly the rows most correlated with a listing or account someone was trying to make disappear.

  **DEVIATION-THAT-IS-A-CORRECTION, same shape as 7.1's UNIQUE columns.** This task's own text and design.md's Data Model prose both say `hidden_by_reports`. `src/shared/db/schema.ts:388` already ships `status: "active" | "expired" | "hidden"` with the comment "`hidden` is the auto-hide state from reports (Phase 8)", and both `markExpired`'s sweep and `DrizzleListingSearch` already filter on those three values. A fourth state would silently break both. This phase uses `hidden`, not `hidden_by_reports` — the schema was already right, the prose was stale.
- [x] 8.2 RED: unauthenticated visitor cannot report. **`src/modules/listing-trust/application/report-listing.test.ts`** — "refuses an anonymous visitor and records nothing" and "reads the session before touching the listing at all", written against `reportListing`/`./report-listing` before that module existed (`Cannot find module`). Same session-gate-first shape `revealContact` already carries. Mutation-checked: swapping the session read and the listing read turns the ordering test red (restored).
- [x] 8.3 RED: 3rd distinct authenticated account triggers auto-hide; repeat report from same account does not. **`src/modules/listing-trust/domain/report-threshold.test.ts`** (pure `resolveReportOutcome`, written before the module existed) plus `report-listing.test.ts`'s "hides the listing when the third distinct reporter arrives" / "records the report and does not hide the listing below the threshold". The distinct count itself is proven for real against Postgres in `tests/integration/listing-trust.test.ts` — the `listing_report_listing_reporter_unique` constraint, not application code, is what makes a repeat report from account A insert zero rows while accounts B and C each insert one.
- [x] 8.4 GREEN: `ReportListingUseCase` — distinct-account counting, `status → hidden` at 3. **`src/modules/listing-trust/application/report-listing.ts`** + `domain/report-threshold.ts`. Two decisions the spec leaves open, made here and recorded rather than left for the code to decide silently:

  **A report on an already-hidden or already-expired listing.** `resolveReportOutcome` only transitions `active → hidden`; a listing that is already `hidden` stays hidden (no "more hidden" state), and an `expired` listing stays `expired` — auto-hiding it would let it escape `markExpired`'s `WHERE status = 'active'` clause forever, which is `tests/integration/lifecycle.test.ts`'s own expiry guarantee breaking through a different module's write path. Both cases still record the report row (never refused, never silently dropped) — only the status transition is a no-op. Proven at the unit level (report-threshold.test.ts, report-listing.test.ts) and against real Postgres (an already-expired listing reported by 3 distinct accounts stays `expired`, `tests/integration/listing-trust.test.ts`).

  **Self-reporting is allowed.** Neither spec scenario exempts the publisher, and the threshold is 3 DISTINCT accounts regardless of who holds one of them — a publisher reporting their own listing spends one of the three seats it takes to hide it and gains nothing an attacker could not already get with a second real account. Covered by "allows the publisher to report their own listing".

  Mutation-checked: `>=` → `>` on the threshold turns both the domain and the application "hides at exactly 3" tests red; removing the `currentStatus !== "active"` guard turns "leaves an expired listing expired" red; removing `.onConflictDoNothing()` from the adapter turns the integration test's repeat-report assertion into a real `23505` unique-violation error instead of a silent no-op. All restored after confirming red.
- [x] 8.5 RED: operator restore returns listing to `active`. **`src/modules/listing-trust/application/restore-listing.test.ts`** (written before `restoreListing` existed) plus **`src/modules/listing-trust/domain/restore-outcome.test.ts`** for the pure decision, and **`src/modules/listing-trust/domain/operator-authorization.test.ts`** for the route's gate — the same seven malformed-header cases `cron-authorization.test.ts` already established, applied to a new `OPERATOR_SECRET` rather than reusing `CRON_SECRET` (two distinct guardians, no reason to share a rotation). All three referenced modules that did not exist yet at write time, confirmed to fail on `Cannot find module` before implementation.
- [x] 8.6 GREEN: `RestoreListingUseCase` + `moderation_action` record; minimal operator-only restore route. **`src/modules/listing-trust/application/restore-listing.ts`**, **`src/modules/listing-trust/infrastructure/drizzle-listing-moderation.ts`** (`DrizzleListingModeration`, `DrizzleListingReports`, `DrizzleModerationActions`), **`src/modules/listing-trust/infrastructure/moderation-config.ts`** (`readOperatorSecret`, same no-default/no-throw shape as `lifecycle-config.ts`), **`app/api/operator/restore-listing/route.ts`** + its `route.test.ts` (mirrors `expiry-reminders/route.test.ts`'s "import the real module, prove the 401/400 paths never touch the database" idiom).

  **"provided it has not also expired" (the spec's own words) is the second precondition, decided in `domain/restore-outcome.ts`.** A `hidden` listing is excluded from `markExpired`'s sweep, so its `expires_at` can sit in the past for as long as it stays hidden. Restoring it straight to `active` would resurrect a listing time has already retired; `resolveRestoreOutcome` instead returns `expired` in that case — still an operator action worth a `moderation_action` row, just not a return to `active`. Proven at the unit level and against real Postgres (`tests/integration/listing-trust.test.ts`, "returns to expired instead of active"). Only a `hidden` listing may be restored at all — `active` or `expired` is refused outright (`ListingNotHiddenError`), never silently no-op'd.

  **The route carries no business decision.** Same split as `sendLifecycleNotices`/`isAuthorizedJobRequest`: `isAuthorizedOperatorRequest` (domain, constant-time, fails closed with no `OPERATOR_SECRET`) gates the route; `restoreListing` itself takes no auth dependency, the same way `renew` and `sendLifecycleNotices` do not know about the cron secret that gates their own route.

  Mutation-checked: removing the `currentStatus !== "hidden"` guard in `resolveRestoreOutcome` turns both "refuses to restore a listing that is not hidden" tests red (domain and application); flipping the expiry comparison turns both "restores... to active"/"returns... to expired" tests red; forcing `isAuthorizedOperatorRequest` to always return `true` turns 3 of 10 `operator-authorization.test.ts` cases red. All restored after confirming red.

- [x] 8.7 **(nueva — encontrada al cerrar la 9.28, no planificada por nadie.)** **`reportListing` no lo llama ninguna ruta.** Es la QUINTA instancia de la misma forma de defecto en este trabajo, y la primera que ninguna tarea nombraba: `src/modules/listing-trust/application/report-listing.ts` está completo y probado (8.2, 8.3, 8.4), `resolveReportOutcome` decide el auto-ocultamiento al tercer reportante distinto, `listing_report` tiene su `UNIQUE(listing_id, reporter_id)` y `tests/integration/listing-trust.test.ts` lo prueba contra Postgres real — y `rg` sobre `app/` y `components/` devuelve **cero** llamadores. En un navegador, nadie puede reportar un aviso, así que el umbral de tres no puede dispararse nunca y el estado `hidden` sólo lo puede producir un `UPDATE` a mano. La lámina 14d ya dibuja la consecuencia («Oculta por reportes») de algo que hoy no puede ocurrir. La Fase 8 no tiene ninguna tarea de interfaz: `restoreListing` sí tiene su ruta de operador (8.6), pero el reporte del inquilino nunca tuvo una. Falta: el disparador en la ficha (`app/alquiler/[ciudad]/[zona]/[slug]/`), su acción de servidor sobre `reportListing`, y la copia del acuse. **No se construye en esta porción**: es Fase 8, no Fase 9, y meterla acá habría mezclado dos alcances en un mismo PR.

  **HECHA.** La puerta es una pantalla propia, `/alquiler/<ciudad>/<zona>/<slug>/reportar`:

  - **`src/modules/listing-trust/domain/report-screen.ts`** — qué se dibuja y qué se dice, decidido donde el suelo de cobertura del 90 % llega. Pruebas: `report-screen.test.ts`, «no tiene por dónde recibir el resultado del reporte» y «dice lo mismo haya ocultado el aviso o no, y no nombra el umbral».
  - **`app/alquiler/[ciudad]/[zona]/[slug]/reportar/page.tsx`** + `reportar.module.css` — el formulario. Prueba: `reportar.test.ts`, «entrega la acción al formulario sin ejecutarla», que afirma las dos mitades a la vez (la acción está cableada Y el render no la llamó).
  - **`app/alquiler/[ciudad]/[zona]/[slug]/reportar/actions.ts`** — la acción de servidor sobre `reportListing`. Pruebas: `actions.test.ts`, «manda al acuse cuando el reporte fue el que ocultó el aviso» y «no reporta nada y manda al inicio con otro origen escrito completo».
  - **`src/modules/identity/domain/safe-return-destination.ts`** gana `safeReturnPath`. Prueba: «rechaza una escapada por segmentos relativos».
  - **`app/alquiler/[ciudad]/[zona]/[slug]/page.tsx`** — el enlace del pie tenía destino y no llevaba a ningún lado: `href="#reportar"` era un ancla a una sección que la ficha nunca dibujó. Prueba: `ficha.test.ts`, «lleva a una ruta que existe de verdad», que afirma contra el sistema de archivos y no contra una cadena.
  - **`tests/integration/listing-report.test.ts`** — la cadena entera contra Postgres real, POR la acción de servidor y no alrededor de ella: «la tercera cuenta distinta oculta el aviso, y el acuse no lo dice». Es lo que faltaba: todo lo que había llamaba a `reportListing` directamente, que es exactamente por qué esta suite no vio el defecto. `vitest.integration.config.ts` gana el alias `@` para poder importar la acción.
  - **`tests/e2e/reportar-aviso.spec.ts`** — «un GET no reporta ni acusa recibo», con pedidos crudos, y corre además en el proyecto `crawlability` con el script apagado. Sin base de datos y sin `test.skip`: el `GET` de esta pantalla no consulta nada.

  **DOS DECISIONES QUE LA LÁMINA NO CONTESTA, tomadas acá y con su razón.**

  1. **Qué se ve después de reportar: un acuse constante.** Decir «este aviso quedó oculto» le entrega a quien ataca el único dato que le falta —cuántas cuentas más necesita—, y no decir nada no le enseña a nadie que el reporte llegó. La salida es la forma que AGENTS.md §7 ya usa para el contacto bloqueado: `ReportSentScreen` **no tiene ningún campo donde el resultado quepa** y `resolveReportScreen` **no tiene ningún parámetro por donde recibirlo**, así que un render no puede filtrarlo. La acción descarta `{ autoHidden }` sin guardarlo ni en una variable, y el destino de la redirección es idéntico en los dos casos.
  2. **El control se le muestra también a quien no tiene sesión.** La lámina dibuja «Reportar este aviso» al pie de una ficha que es anónima, y la spec de listing-trust describe al visitante sin sesión *intentando* reportar («the system blocks the action and requires sign-in first») — no se puede intentar lo que no se ve. Esconderlo haría que nadie descubriera que reportar existe, y reportar es la única señal antifraude que un inquilino puede dar. El muro está en la acción, no en el render, y devuelve a la pantalla de reportar y no a la ficha: quien entró para reportar sigue queriendo reportar.

  **DESVÍOS, registrados en vez de decididos en silencio.**

  - **Pantalla propia y no un bloque al pie de la ficha**, aunque el `href="#reportar"` que había sugería lo segundo. Sin JavaScript el acuse sólo puede llegar por una URL, así que reportar termina en una redirección; hacerla contra la ficha publicaría variantes con parámetro de la página más indexada del sitio para decir «gracias». La pantalla propia lleva `noindex` honesto.
  - **No arrastra el origen de la búsqueda** (`desde`, 16.9). Es un desvío sin salida cuya única puerta es volver al aviso, y el botón de atrás del navegador devuelve la URL de la ficha con su origen intacto; enhebrarlo además por el POST habría sido llevar estado a través de una redirección para un enlace al que se llega apretando atrás.
  - **`safeReturnPath` vive en `identity/domain` junto a `safeSignInDestination`** y comparte su origen inventado y su prefijo `/alquiler/`. Devuelve `null` en vez de un respaldo: acá el valor se concatena para armar `…/reportar?enviado`, y un respaldo silencioso convertiría una ruta hostil en un acuse dibujado sobre una pantalla que no es la nuestra.

  **CONTRADICCIÓN ENTRE PLAN Y CÓDIGO, no arreglada acá.** El texto de la 8.7 —y también el de la 8.3 y la 8.4— cita `tests/integration/listing-trust.test.ts`. Ese archivo no existe: las pruebas de integración están en `tests/integration/listing-report.test.ts` y `tests/integration/listing-restore.test.ts`. Se deja dicho para que la próxima persona no lo busque.

  **CONTRADICCIÓN ENTRE LÁMINA Y ESPECIFICACIÓN.** La especificación de la ficha (§2, paso 10) dice «Formulario de reporte», o sea una pantalla; las dos láminas de la ficha enlazan «Reportar este aviso» a **su propio `id`** (`#10a` en móvil, `#11d` en escritorio), así que la referencia declara el enlace y no dibuja la pantalla que abre. Ninguna lámina de reportar existe. Se derivó del sistema, como AGENTS.md §2 manda para las 16 superficies sin lámina: `FormShell` (una columna de 600px), la tipografía de la ficha, `ActionButton`, y ni un valor literal.

  **Mutaciones confirmadas, cada una con la prueba que puso en rojo** (aplicadas con un script que exige que el texto objetivo aparezca exactamente una vez y que el hash del archivo cambie — `sd` no reemplaza literales multilínea y sale 0):

  | Mutación | Prueba que se puso en rojo |
  |---|---|
  | `sentFlag === undefined ? FORM : SENT` → `sentFlag ? SENT : FORM` | «con el parámetro pelado, sin valor dibuja el acuse» |
  | `resolveReportScreen` gana un segundo parámetro | «no tiene por dónde recibir el resultado del reporte» |
  | el acuse dice «y el aviso quedó oculto» | «dice lo mismo haya ocultado el aviso o no…» |
  | `safeReturnPath` deja de mirar el origen | «rechaza otro origen escrito completo» (+2) |
  | el prefijo se compara sobre el texto crudo | «rechaza una escapada por segmentos relativos» |
  | se quita el `catch` de `safeReturnPath` | «rechaza un host que hace lanzar al parser» |
  | la redirección lleva `&oculto=${autoHidden}` | «manda al acuse…» (los dos casos) y «no distingue un caso del otro por el destino» |
  | la vuelta deja de pasar por `safeReturnPath` | «no reporta nada y manda al inicio…» (4 casos) |
  | sin sesión vuelve a la ficha en vez de a reportar | «manda a entrar con la vuelta a la pantalla de reportar» |
  | la página llama a la acción al dibujarse | «entrega la acción al formulario sin ejecutarla» |
  | el formulario se queda sin `action` | «entrega la acción al formulario sin ejecutarla» |
  | se quita la guarda `listingIdFromSlug` | «no dibuja el formulario» |
  | el enlace del pie apunta a `/reportes` | «lleva a una ruta que existe de verdad» |
  | la acción lee `formData.get("aviso")` | integración: «el primer reportante deja una fila…» (+3) |

  **Presupuesto excedido y dicho, no callado**: ~1.160 líneas de autoría (~430 de implementación, ~730 de pruebas y configuración) contra las 400 del presupuesto. No es divisible sin volver a crear el defecto: cualquier corte entrega o una pantalla sin acción o una acción sin pantalla, que es exactamente la forma de la 8.7.

- [ ] 8.8 **(nueva — consecuencia directa de la 8.7, no un defecto de ella.)** **Un aviso ocultado por reportes sólo se puede restaurar con `curl`.** `restoreListing` está completo (8.6) y su única puerta es `app/api/operator/restore-listing/route.ts`, detrás de un `OPERATOR_SECRET` de portador — sin pantalla, sin sesión de operador y sin modelo de cuenta que la sostenga (8.1 explica por qué `moderation_action` no tiene columna `actor`). Mientras nadie podía reportar, eso era teoría: el estado `hidden` no lo producía nada. **Desde la 8.7 sí lo produce**, y tres cuentas cualesquiera pueden ocultar un aviso legítimo, así que la restauración pasa a ser un camino que se va a recorrer. Falta decidir —es una decisión del fundador, no una técnica— si el operador tiene pantalla o si el `curl` alcanza para el MVP; y, si tiene pantalla, hace falta primero un modelo de cuenta de operador, que hoy no existe. `/mis-avisos` ya dibuja el estado («Ocultas», `publisher-listing-board.ts`), así que quien publica **sí** se entera; lo que no tiene es a quién pedirle la revisión.

  **Ampliada por el fundador (2026-08-27): no es un trabajo de operador, son DOS, y hoy se hacen de formas distintas.** Habilitar la carga masiva de una agencia es el otro: `user.bulk_import_enabled` se lee en cuatro lugares y **no lo escribe ni una línea de código**, así que prenderlo es un `UPDATE` a mano contra Neon. Restaurar, en cambio, es un `curl` con secreto de portador. Dos herramientas, dos maneras de equivocarse — y una asimetría que importa: restaurar deja constancia en `moderation_action`, el `UPDATE` no deja nada. Lo que se decida acá cubre las dos, para no construir una pantalla de restauración y descubrir después que falta la otra.

  **Lo que NO se toca, confirmado por el fundador el mismo día:** toda cuenta nueva nace con la bandera en `false`. Ya está garantizado en tres capas —`NOT NULL DEFAULT false` en el esquema, cero escrituras en el código, y el único `true` (`app/importar/page.tsx:63`) es para dibujar el menú DESPUÉS de que `isBulkImportAuthorized` dejó pasar, nunca para otorgar. Es la regla que el propio esquema escribe: **«operator-set, never self-service»**, sin ningún default que convierta un hueco en un permiso.

- [x] 8.9 **(nueva — encontrada barriendo en busca de una sexta instancia de la 8.7.)** **Cerrada 2026-08-28: los tres se borraron, y la prueba de que no dibujaban nada es el paquete.** Antes de tocar nada se estableció en positivo y no por `grep`: no hay archivo barril en `app/` ni en `components/`, los cuatro `import(` dinámicos del repositorio son `sharp-dhash` y tres `await import("./route")` de pruebas, y las cinco apariciones de `CityZoneSelect` fuera de sus dos archivos son **comentarios** (`catalogue.ts`, `seed.ts`, `search-criteria.ts` ×2). Medición del paquete con `pnpm build && pnpm budget:bundle` antes y después: **once de las doce rutas quedan byte a byte idénticas**; la única que se mueve es la ficha, 106.43 → 106.42 KB gzip, y esa diferencia **se aisló midiendo, no suponiendo** — restaurando sólo las reglas `.row` de `Field.module.css` la ficha vuelve a 106.43 exacto. O sea que los diez bytes son el mapa de clases de esa hoja (la ficha dibuja `ContactBlock`, que dibuja `Field`) y **los tres componentes borrados aportaban cero**. Por qué cada uno sobraba: `PageShell` nunca se cableó —su propio comentario decía que no entraba en `app/layout.tsx` para no anidar dos `<main>`— y cada pantalla resolvió sus landmarks a mano; `CityZoneSelect` quedó superado por `SearchFilters`, que ya lleva la regla D5 adentro (`zonesForCity`, misma función); y `FieldRow` existía para el par ciudad/zona del `PublishForm` que la 3.9(c) reemplazó por `PublishStep`, **que no pregunta la ciudad en ningún paso** (medido: `layout.spec.ts` → «3.9: la ciudad no se pregunta en ninguna parte del paso»), así que el par para el que existía no puede existir. Se van con ellos `CityZoneSelect.module.css`, las reglas `.row`/`.row > *` de `Field.module.css` y las dos suites de sus componentes; la aserción «pairs two fields on one row at every width» se borra **con** su sujeto y no se reapunta a otra hoja, por la misma razón que la 14.42 dejó escrita en `design-contract.test.tsx`. Verificado por `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` y `vitest run` (1.870 pruebas, 8 menos: las que medían lo borrado).

  **Contradicción anotada al pasar, no arreglada acá:** el texto de la 3.9 dice que «el patrón de campo se mudó a `components/molecules/Field.tsx`» para que la próxima pantalla partiera de las reglas. Hoy `Field` tiene **un solo** consumidor y no es el formulario de publicar: es `ContactBlock`. El `PublishStep` que reemplazó al `PublishForm` dibuja sus campos con `publish-steps.module.css` y no importa `Field`.

  ~~**(nueva — encontrada barriendo en busca de una sexta instancia de la 8.7.)**~~ **Tres componentes exportados que no dibuja nadie**: `PageShell` (`components/layout/PageShell.tsx`), `CityZoneSelect` (`components/molecules/CityZoneSelect.tsx`) y `FieldRow` (`components/molecules/Field.tsx`) no aparecen en ningún archivo servido de `app/` ni de `components/`. **No es la misma forma de defecto que la 8.7 y conviene no confundirlas**: un caso de uso inalcanzable es una capacidad del producto que no existe; un componente huérfano es una pantalla que dibujó lo suyo a mano en vez de componerlo, o código que sobró de un rediseño. `CityZoneSelect` es el interesante: publicar y buscar eligen ciudad y zona, así que o lo deberían estar usando o el molécula sobra. La barrida de `a02e1b1` («borrar lo que el nav y el modal dejaron sin quien los dibuje») ya hizo una pasada de esta clase; estos tres quedaron.

  **Lo que esa misma barrida SÍ descartó, y vale registrarlo**: no hay una sexta instancia de la 8.7. Los 22 casos de uso de `src/modules/*/application/` tienen llamador —`collect-curated-zones` y `process-uploaded-photo` llegan indirectamente, por `run-import-validation` y por `app/mis-avisos/actions.ts`— y las siete funciones de dominio exportadas que no aparecen fuera de su propio test (`parsePhotoNumber`, `photoNeighbours`, `safeResultsOrigin`, `completedSteps`, `pageCount`, `readSearchStep`, `acceptsZoneQuery`) las usa otra función del mismo archivo que sí tiene llamador. `reportListing` era el último.

## Phase 9: Broker Bulk Import (PR9)

Depends on PR3 (publication use cases) and PR4 (trust pipeline). Creates no write path of its own into `listing`.

- [x] 9.1 Schema: `user.bulk_import_enabled` (bool, default false); `listing.external_reference` (nullable); UNIQUE `(publisher_id, external_reference)`; `listing.status` gains `draft`; `bulk_import_batch` table. **`src/shared/db/schema.ts`, migration `drizzle/0014_fast_zaran.sql`.** Proven against real Postgres by `tests/integration/broker-bulk-import.test.ts` ("listing.status acepta 'draft'", "dos avisos del mismo publisher con la misma referencia externa violan la restricción única" — code `23505`, and the NULL-does-not-collide case). **Correction beyond the task text, recorded per AGENTS.md §5:** `bulk_import_batch` ships as a minimal scaffold (`id`, `publisher_id`, `uploaded_at`, `total_rows`, `valid_rows`, `created_drafts`) — the task only asked for the table to exist; its full shape belongs to `ValidateImportUseCase`/`ConfirmImportUseCase` (9.13-9.17), which no task before 9.1 has designed yet. **Also touched, and why it was safe:** `listing.status`'s TS union widened to include `draft`, which broke two *independent* narrower unions that read the same column — `ListingModerationStatus` (`src/modules/listing-trust/domain/report-threshold.ts`) and `RenewableListing.status` (`src/modules/listing-lifecycle/application/ports/lifecycle-listings.port.ts`). Both were widened alongside it with a comment explaining why no new branch is needed (each already fails closed via a `!== "active"`/`!== "hidden"` guard). `pnpm typecheck` was clean both before this widening (confirming the two holes above were the only ones) and after (confirming the fix). See the risk map in this apply's return summary for every place that reasons about `listing.status` and what `draft` means there today.

Every future import endpoint (CSV upload, confirm, photo attach — 9.7+) MUST call `authorizeBulkImport` first rather than re-checking the column itself.

Also proven at the composed acceptance layer: **`src/modules/broker-bulk-import/application/parse-import-file.test.ts`** ("rejects the WHOLE file when precio_usd is missing... before any row is processed").
- [x] 9.5 RED: semicolon-delimited file carrying a UTF-8 BOM parses into correct columns, not one column. **`src/modules/broker-bulk-import/domain/csv-import-text.test.ts`** (`stripBom`, `sniffDelimiter`) + **`src/modules/broker-bulk-import/domain/csv-import-rows.test.ts`** ("splits a semicolon-delimited file into CORRECT columns, not one column"), composed and proven end-to-end in **`parse-import-file.test.ts`** ("parses a semicolon-delimited file carrying a UTF-8 BOM into CORRECT COLUMNS, not one column").
- [x] 9.6 RED: non-UTF-8 file rejected with a message telling the broker to re-export as CSV UTF-8. **`src/modules/broker-bulk-import/domain/decode-csv-bytes.test.ts`** (strict `TextDecoder({fatal:true})`, refuses an invalid byte sequence) + **`parse-import-file.test.ts`** ("rejects a file that cannot be decoded as UTF-8, telling the broker to re-export as CSV UTF-8" — asserts the thrown `ImportEncodingError`'s message matches `/CSV UTF-8/`).
- [x] 9.7 GREEN: CSV parser — delimiter sniffing (`,` / `;`), BOM strip, UTF-8 enforcement, streaming read. **`src/modules/broker-bulk-import/domain/csv-import-text.ts`** (BOM strip, delimiter sniff outside quoted regions) **+ `decode-csv-bytes.ts`** (strict UTF-8) **+ `csv-import-rows.ts`** (RFC4180-ish tokenizer: quoted fields, escaped `""`, CRLF tolerance) **→ `src/modules/broker-bulk-import/application/read-bounded-import-file.ts`** (the streaming half: reads `ImportFileSourcePort.chunks()` via `for await`, never buffers past a crossed bound) **→ `parse-import-file.ts`** (orchestrates all of it). **Correction beyond the task text, recorded per AGENTS.md §5:** "streaming read" is honest only up to the point bounds are enforced — once a file is proven ≤2 MB / ≤50 rows, the (necessarily small) remainder is decoded and tokenized in memory, which is standard and was never claimed to be streamed further. The genuinely streamed, no-buffer part is exactly where it matters: an oversized file is never read to completion (proven by `read-bounded-import-file.test.ts` asserting `consumedChunks()` stays far below the total chunk count on an ~8 MB adversarial input). **A deliberate simplification, stated rather than claimed away:** a CSV field may not contain a literal newline, even quoted — every `\n`/`\r\n` is a row separator. This is what keeps the streaming newline-count and the parser's real row count in exact agreement (see 9.8/9.9); a broker portfolio's `titulo`/`descripcion` cells are single-line copy, so this costs nothing real. Documented at the top of `csv-import-rows.test.ts`.
- [x] 9.8 RED: file exceeding the row or size limit is refused before its contents are parsed. **`src/modules/broker-bulk-import/domain/csv-import-bounds.test.ts`** + **`src/modules/broker-bulk-import/application/read-bounded-import-file.test.ts`** — every case proves the guarantee TWO ways: the right error is thrown, AND the fake source's `consumedChunks()` proves the stream was never read to completion (e.g. "refuses a file whose DECLARED size already exceeds the limit, WITHOUT reading a single chunk" — 0 chunks consumed; the ~8 MB adversarial-declared-size case aborts having consumed under half the chunks).
- [x] 9.9 GREEN: size and row bounds enforced pre-parse. **`src/modules/broker-bulk-import/domain/csv-import-bounds.ts`** (`MAX_IMPORT_FILE_SIZE_BYTES = 2 MB`, `MAX_IMPORT_ROW_COUNT = 50`, pure `checkFileSizeBound`/`checkRowCountBound` over numbers, no I/O) **→ `read-bounded-import-file.ts`** (streaming enforcement: `declaredByteLength` checked before a single chunk is read; actual bytes-read and newline-count both tracked live and abort the `for await` loop the instant either is CERTAIN to exceed the bound — defends against a lying declared size) **→ `parse-import-file.ts`** (an authoritative post-parse row-count check against the real parsed row count, for the boundary case a streaming newline-count alone cannot always resolve, e.g. exactly 51 rows with no trailing newline — proven by `parse-import-file.test.ts`'s dedicated boundary test). Both refusal messages name the limit (2 MB / 50 rows), matching the founder's requirement that a broker learns to split the file rather than stare at a generic error. **Mutation-checked**: disabling the size-bound check does not merely fail an assertion — it crashes the test worker with an out-of-memory error on the adversarial-declared-size test, which is itself strong proof the guard is load-bearing exactly where it matters.

The vocabulary is decided and reusable now so that use case never re-invents it.
